           and face of a man and a date.    I el,*4rAit look4 at it, brat threw the

           coin as far as I could back into the dust.      I left the ruin.     Why
           did I throw away the coin?    I don't know.    I was not afraid, but

           I had to leave that spot without the coin.
 ^

               "At night I park my car at my C.I.G. Headquarters at Villa

           Borghese.    I never let the guards drive me to the hotel.       I want     ()

                                                                                       ()
           Via Sicilia, often at 2:00 or 3:00 a.m.      Foolish perhaps, but I
                                                                                       ()
           can't resist wandering the streets in the early morning hours,               CD
           alone and when the streets are deserted.      I never remember
                                                                                       0

                                                                                      ()
           Sicilia.    A man stepped in front of me.   I don't know where he           0

                                                                                      0

                                  instant, when we went after him and rode him down

                                  I looked at the Chief of Police, thinking now I ruined

                                  everything.    He will think I am Crazy. j But, he said, "I think

                                  I understand.    In a few days you will be notified.   A grateful

                                  Italy is awarding you the first and highest honor ever given

                                  a foreigner.    You will receive the "Italian Was Cross". We

                                  decided you are one of ours."

                                       (At the ceremony I looks on the "Croce de Guerra" and saw

                                  inscribed on the back, "For Military Valor".    I asked General

                                  Cardonna, the Italian Chief of Staff. "Isn't this a mistake,

                                  shouldn't it be for meritorious achievement " He answered, "No,

                                  we made no mistake, it is inscribed correctly."

                                       Years later, the adjutant General of the United States

                                  Army issued and order that I was authorized to officially accept

                                  the "Italian War Cross" and the "Order of the Crown of Italy"

                                  and wear them with my other decorations.)

                                       At our next session Chief Ferrari, said "Corso, my men -

                                  reported to me a dead body of a Gestapo agent.     He was cut with

                                  a knife and shot.    Can you tell me what happened. I said "Yes

                                  Sir, I have nothing to hide from you. I am not sure what

                                  happened. With an uncanny instinct and not even thinking, I

                                  drew my knife.    My assailant was about ten feet away.   It seemed
                                  that the knife leaped and drew me with it.     It found is mark
                                  and seems without my assistance.    This is the unreal part of
                                  the story for which I can offer no
                                                                     explanation, except perhaps
                                  I am a survivor    Now I will relate the real part of the

          One night, coming out of a Roman night club, a man

     approached on my right. Wi4h4.4414;ntam1/414y instinct, I sensed his
                                   N1/4

     mission. 7,fre devil's knife flashed, it cut into his right

     forearm, and opened it like a fish's belly.                          I could feel it
     slicing through the soft flesh.                 With my left hand, I reached for
     the gun he held in his now limp, nerveless fingers.                         I fired, and
     he crumpled.       I turned to the doorman and said, "Call the Italian

     police to clean up this garbage, and tell them to send any papers

     on him to .me."         He saluted, and said, "Si Senor."                 I put the gun,
     a 9mm Beretta, in my pocket.               The gun intended to kill me, I

     carried it for the. duration of my stay in Rome.                        It became the

     companion of the devil's knife which had made its first mark on a

     killer.

     ripped his- forearm as clean as a razor cut.)                        This became the

     deadly sign of the devil's knife, for which it was well suited.

           1            We both began to laugh.   I never knew whether or not the old

               man was a serious romantic or merely jesting with me, but I loved
      4-1      him for his attitude and said, "When I leave, the dagger will

      We went through many traAls together.     One of the most

 important was the counterfeiting of the new issue of Italian

 money.     One night the head waiter at one of the nightclub

 restaurants, on.my payroll, overheard two Yugoslavia cOmmunist

 diplomats, who had been drinking heavily -- that once the

. Italians released their new issue of Italian money (lire), they

 would ruin the economy by flooding the country with virtually

 undetectable counterfeits.    This was not unusual, since I had

 broken up a, ring of counterfeiters making our script.   The Nazis

 had duplicated the British 20 pound note so perfectly that the


British had to retire the entire issue. The favorite of black

marketeers was the British 5 pound sterling gold coin.    The fake

was 24 carat gold, while the original was 22 carats with a

hardener.    It brought such large amounts of paper money that it

paid the counterfeiters to make the 24 carat facsimile, which was

more valuable than the original.

     I lesseett'irta went' to the Office of the Chief of Police. I
      Excttatevcr
asked gel-tort, "Is the government printing a new issue of lire?"

He got up and said, "My God, Corso don't say anything further."

He closed all the doors and sat close to me and said, "This is

supposedly the closest held secret in the government."

     I gave him the information I had received.    He shook his

head in anguish and said I must notify the Prime Minister.        We

did, and then returned to his office.    I told him, "Excellency,

we must muster all our forces; since I can reach out farther

Austria, Yugoslavia, etc.   Will you trust me to head the

investigation?"   He answered, "Yes, I am sure the Prime Minister

will approve."

     I asked, "I know you have never joined forces, but I want

General De Pasquale of S.I.M. to help.   May I call him?"    He

said, "Yes, you are in charge." I called the General.      He came

immediately.   I called Moreno, my head of C.I.C., and told him to

bring Leo Pagnotta, our best C.I.C. officer.   I asked General

De Pasquale to assign me one of his Majors, who was known as the

best investigator in the Italian service; and. I asked Chief

Ferrari to give me the head of his "Celere" Lightning Squad. Both

men were sent for immediately.   I had the three best

investigators in Italy, with Leo Pagnotta, whom they respected,

      gold, brought in diplomatic pouches.          I hit upon a scheme to         ()

      Vienna, (an old friend), and asked him to send me all the Russian E.-‘

'7n   rubles he could get his hands on.

      courier delivered 50,000'rubles.

                                                                             C)

                            The day of the sale there was frantic scurrying         0
      into the night.
                                                                                    0

 CD                Soon after I completed the investigation on the Communist
 CD
              counterfeiting of the new issue of Italian money, whdich.-was,never
 C)
 CD           placed - in circulation -end Had- to- be' destroyed, I was paid a visit
 CD           by the Italian Minister of Finance.        He was a distinguished,
 CD           scholarly gentleman in his 70's.      He was a former Professor of
CD
              Economics and known for his brilliance in monetary matters.           He
CD
CD
CD
       ii     had been appointed to bring Italy out of her post-war fiscal

              chaos.
CD                 When I received a call from my reception desk that he had
CD
CD            come to visit me, I, personally, went down to escort him to mV

C)            .office.   Although I was the top security officer in the Rome
CD
       ELI    area, I made it a policy to call on high-ranking officials in

              their offices.      For instance, they were the Chief of Police of

              Italy, the Chief of Italian Intelligence, etc., who were men in


       7:1    their late 50's or 60's.      I mas 28.    In deference to their age,

              if nothing else, I felt I should cell on them rather than have

              them come to me.     I later discovered that this was deeply

              appreciated, since it enhanced their position and prestige with

              their staffs and others because of the position that I held.

                   When the Minister of Finance called on me, I realized that

              this must be a personal visit'rather than an official one.        When

              we settled in my office, .he began:       "I wish to thank you for what

              you did for my country and for me personally."        He continued.

              "The new issue of lire was my idea and, but for you, could have

              ended in disaster."
            I responded, "I cannot see how, under the circumstances, it
       could have reflected on you; but I can understand your feelings.

       With Fascist and German printing of the lire, even I understand
       that the present paper money in circulation must be recalled.

       Although your time table has been disrupted, you have to print a

       new issue.   I was most fortunate to obtain the information which

       led to the investigation...

          • Then I concluded, "But, Senor Minister, who am I to tell you

       about finances?   You could teach .me much that I don't know

       concerning money.    I would be host appreciative if, from time to

       time, I could come to you and learn. I will not impose too much

       on your time, but could you take a.novice. pupil under your wing?"()

            His answer was typical, "I owe you much more than that; but .0

       I would be most proud, to teach you the intricacies of money."       C)
            And so, I was most fortunate to learn a new subject, on         0
                                                                            C)
       which I knew little or nothing, from one of the world's premier

       scholars on the subject and which, in the future, stood in good      C)
       stance to understand world events and why they happened.

           (Because of what he taught me, years later I was able to warns

       two friends, months ahead of time, on €he fiasco which was about 3

       to happen and was given the label "Watergate)" he told me.


1   We concluded the session.

         At our next session, I began as follows: "Senor Minister,

    not too long ago I had a meeting with Giussepi saragat, the

    right-wing socialist.   He describes, almost identically that

             "Du Beers controls the price of diamonds by

        the supply on the world market. This is a relatively
                                                             small
        operation in comparison to the money and gold markets.
                                                               The
        diamond    price control is widely accepted, since it is a luxury

        and not a necessity.


11          Have you ever wondered who sets the price, or how

        of gold or money is arrived at on a daily basis.
                                                              the value

                                                             Remember these
        are not arbitrary figures, or come out of thin air.     They are
        set daily by individuals as real as you and I.     This practice
       is widely accepted as a necessity.
rs
            "Imagine what you could become if you knew the price of

       gold tomorrow, or the price of the dollar, franc, lira, mark,

 TI    etc. 24 hours from now. Those that practice this power are,

       in effect governing in an international scale.      They control

       the value of money in your pocket.    They institute controls

       which can stop the growth of an institute controls which can

       stop the growth of an enterprise or make it grow.

            I stopped him here and said, "Free enterprise and individual

       initative are dependent in the function of this system."      He

       said, "Yes, with certain qualifications.    The answer to your

       question.    By raising or lowering interest rates and loosing

       or tightening money supply.    Can effect their success or demise.

       not too long ago I had a

                                           1.


a Socialist Government operates in the same manner as the 1

International Bankers.

     The minister answered, "You are learning".    A socialist

government can make an enterprise prosper or retard it.        A bank

can make a business grow or stop its credit and it will flounder.

    I cane back with, "What about the    USSR.   He respond, "They

have no problem with the USSR.   All international Soviet trade

is handled in large banks in Stockholm and New York. The Soviets

play by the rules.   Even when they sell gold, they are very

careful to fit into the London-Zurich game plan."

    He continued, "The would monetary system, 30 years ago,

was implanted in the Unites States.   It is called the Federal

Reserve System.   In the future, if you pay attention you will

see its effects on the wogld, tight money, high interest rates,

set as a powerful group desires up or down.     They are not

answerable to the public and their every action is secret.

     I learned much, much more from the old minister, but if

put in print, it will sound so fantastic that the facts tend

to lose their credibility.   However, I did learn enough and

was able to apply certain factors to select events which greatly

enhanced my "Evaluation" process and broadened the scope of

my intelligence findings, without mentioning money which is

mostly misunderstood or misapplied.

        The only time I was admonished by General Dasher was soon
               A
after kliats raid.                     He found out I had been going on raids with my

men.      He said he no longer wanted me to go with them.

       I said, "But General, I can't order them to risk their lives

and not take the same chances.                                        I know you understand."

       "So you have been checking on me," he stated, "And know I
used to jump with my men."

       "Yes, Sir, and they still talk about you."

       He concluded, "The nature of your business, perhaps, often

requires your presence; but I forbid you to take unnecessary

chances, and remember, you are the prime target."

0                     I told my men that our exposed rear ends were now covered

                    and we could begin concentrating on Nazi 'agents, saboteurs and

                    collaborators:
 "STAY BEHIND NETS"


      The Germans, when they evacuated Rome early in June, 1944,    C)
                                                                    C)
 had no way of knowing' that the war would end with a German defeat c)

 eleven months later.    The goal, in their retreat into northern
                                                                       C)
 Italy, and later into Austria was to delay the allied advance and

 force the Allies to pay as high a price as possible for all of

 the land they took.    In these actions, they were quite

 successful, and there were no rapid advances comparable to

 General Patton's spectacular drive across France in the late

 summer and fall of 1944.  It was a slow, tough, foot—slogging
                                  it OND
 drive to the Alps with bitter hand Alighting all the way.

      Meanwhile, back in Rome, it soon was evident that the

 Germans had not evacuated the city as completely as we had

 initially thought.

      The Nazis, as they fled north, left what we called, "stay

 behind nets" of spies and saboteurs.     One such spy net was

 sending back coded information on Allied troop movements and

 other pertinent military intelligence.    The British FSC brought

 in a truck which contained a radio direction finder.    We were

 able to isolate the transmitter location to a large apartment or

 tenement building.    It contained hundreds of apartments.   We

".could not pinpoint the apartment from which the radio was

 transmitting.   If we raided or began a search, the spies we knew

 were alert and would move or lose themselves in the hundreds of

 people in the building.

      Finally, we got the needed break.    The spies needed crystals
        for their radios, and a courier would deliver them to a contact

        at a designated spot in the Villa Borghese Park in Rome.

        Fortunately, it was in a "lovers lane" area.      We did not have

        enough Italian girls working at our C.I.C., so some of the agents

        had to pair off with C.I.C. agents dressed as women.       This watch

        went on for a few days.    Finally, the courier was spotted passing

fj      a package to a contact.    Both were apprehended at gun point,

        since both, as expected, were armed.
)1              Under some pressure, the contact man told us the apartment
la

11      number.    (The pressure had to be applied quickly and the contact

        made to talk. because, with any delay, our quarry would flee.)       We

        quickly raided the 3rd floor apartment.      One of the two operators
r :-

        jumped from a window and broke his leg, but survived, and was
hi 1

        captured by the waiting agents.

                We had a golden opportunity.   The radio was intact.   We had

        the three operators intact (except for the broken leg) and the

        code book and the apartment.    A decision was made to play them

        back.    (The British were a great help in this venture, since we

        were amateurs.)    Fake troop and supply movements were quickly

        formulated.     The Italians had done a similar operation against

        the Yugoslays, in Yugoslavia, a few years earlier.     The Chief of

        Italian Intelligence advised me that a Morse code radio

       • operator's "touch" is like a fingerprint and cannot be

        duplicated.    Just before the operation began, I spoke to the two

       enemy spies and told them, "You are going to be shot as spies.

       You have caused us considerable damage.      However, you have one

       chance to stay alive.     We have a monitor on the radio.    If we

        suspect, and I emphasized, even suspect, that you try to warn the
                                                                         C)
                                                                          0
                                                                         10


                                                                         0
                                                                         0
Germans that we are playing you back, you will be shot       •

immediately.    You know who I am, and that I can keep my word           ()
either way."                                                             ()
                                                                          C)
     They answered, "Yes, we were warned against you.       Now we are
                                                                          C)
relieved that you caught us, and we will cooperate fully."
                                                                          C)
     They did, and for almost three weeks we sent back false             ()
information.    This gave us a great advantage while the German          ()
                                                                         ()
armies were fleeing north.    After three weeks, the operation was
                                                                         ()
abruptly stopped, since now it could become counter productive.           C)
A quick cutoff was a natural'occurrence, since the Germans knew           0

such agents were expendable.     The spies were not shot.                ()
                                                                         ()
     Next, we received a break, but inherited a most perplexing
                                                                          ()
situation.     A "stay behind" net of twelve saboteurs armed with        -0
explosives.    The surrendering was one of the twelve, but did not        C)
                                                                          ()
know the identity of the other eleven.     The case was assigned to
                                                                          ()
one of my best agents, Leo Pagnotta, an ex —Boston detective.       He
                                                                          ()
was given the twelve Italian custom guards and any other agents           ()
that he needed.    Then the painstaking operation began.    We began      0
                                                                             C)
to solve it like a mathematical equation.    First, the known
                                                                             0
quantity, the agent who had surrendered.    He agreed to stay in             0
place.   He was of utmost importance.   He led to the second                 C)

contact, and now there were ten.    In quick succession two more             C

were caught.    The fourth one led us to a cache of explosives, and

we netted two more.    (These priioners were not kept in my

security prison, but in isolation in the Italian Custom prison

and did not appear on my arrest sheets.)     A money exchange in the

black market- netted one more.   The Italian customs surveillance

team netted another and we were two-thirds of the way home.
000000000000000 JOU
                                 Another explosive cache and a shootout netted two more,-and the

                                 last two with the large cache of explosives ended the case.

                                        At the trail in a United States - British Military Court,

                                 the President stated that the defendants did not look

                                 particularly dangerous.     At this point I asked my agents to bring

                                 in some of the explosives.     We were quickly told to get them out

                                 of the courtroom.    Five were found guilty and executed before

                                 United Stated Military Police firing squads.       The others were

                                 interned, and two were granted a delayed amnesty.       The guilty had

                                 blown up our gasoline pipe lines outside of Rome, which was an

                                 act of sabotage punishable by firing squad.


    0000000000000000000000000
                                        Although I had been in fighting during the Salerno, Cassino,

                                 Anzio campaigns, witnessing an execution was an unforgettable

                                 experience.    The first time we shot two saboteurs, the officer in

                                 charge gave the order too quickly, and one man did not die

                                 immediately.    The officer hesitated giving the coup de grace.

                                 •The next execution was three men.    This time the officer' in

                                ' charge was most efficient and walked right up with his .45 pistol

                                 and administered the coup de grace.       Later that night I saw him

                                •at the bar drinking straight shots of whiskey,. obviously with no

                                 effect.    I went up to him in an attempt to console him with small

                                 talk that I had caught them, prepared the case, wanted them shot,

                                 etc.    'I was drinking along with him.   Finally, I realized that I

                                •.was having no effect on him or myself.     The straight shots of

                                 whiskey or alcohol could not penetrate the emotional barrier we

                                 had built up mentally and in our nervous system, to steel us

                                 against witnessing an execution of human tieings.

                                        As fast as we rqunded 'UP: their nets and agents, the Germans
     kept throwing more at us.    One morning at 4:00 a.m., my phone

     rang.    A German airplane had dropped a box with four parachutes

     attached, on the outskirts of Rome.    (This was a common practice

     which kept their agents and equipment together.)     We quickly

     surrounded the area, found the box and parachutes and caught two

     of the agents.    The third man with the explosives eluded our net.

     The next few days netted us little.    I did get a call from the

     prison that one of the men was the obvious leader, but he was

     arrogant, intelligent and looked at my interrogator with contempt

     in his eyes.    I told them, "Let him simmer.   I will be down

     tomorrow.   Put him in an empty cell with a small table and a

     chair on either side." The next day I put on my uniform and went

     to the prison.    I told my C.I.C. agents to lock the door after me

     and went in the cell.    The prisoner was sitting in one of the

     chairs; I sat in the other across from him.     We stared at each

Li   other for a few minutes, and he stared back in contempt.    (I

     figured that I had every advantage; he was in prison, facing

     death.   All I had to do was break his strong will.) -

          Finally, I said, "In 48 hours you will be dead!     I am your

     only hope."    With this statement of twelve words I felt I would

     use his intellect and strong will against him. I planted the word

     death and the impossible solution of where he would be at the end

     of 48 hours — dead!   The choice was his.

          We stared at each other for about twenty minutes.    Suddenly,

     he began to get a film of liquid over his eyes, or glassy eyes.

     This showed me that the emotion was building up.    (The British

     had trained me in interrogation.)

          All at once he blurted out, "I didn't lie to you!!!"
     :PLANNE,D.RATUS


           With the surrender of the German armies May 8, 1945, we

     began to get a large influx of Gestapo, SS and just plain German

     soldiers.    Either they didn't want to go home or were running

     from their crimes.    My prison population showed a sharp rise. To

     this point I had made about 2,000 arrests.

          One of my informants told me one day that he had tailed two

     obvious German military.    He was sure because they walked

     throwing their, legs back in a stiff motion.   I put a watch :on the

     building.    From the information derived, I suspected they were

     high - ranking officers -- probably Gestapo.    I decided to pick

11   them up.

          The morning of the raid, one came out of the building and

     headed to his left.    The second went to his right.   As they

     reached the corner, they were tapped on the back of the head by a

     revolver barrel, held by one of my agents stepping out of a

 L   doorway.    They raised their hands in surrender.   They both were

     Gestapo Colonels.
:1
          Later I asked them, "You were heavily armed; why didn't you

     resist?"

     •    They answered, "The moment we got the tap from behind, we

     realized the streets were deserted and you had us trapped."      One

     of them continued, "How many guns did you have on us?"

          I told him, "Twenty on each of you.   And, of course, you

     know you only had three seconds to get your hands up."

          The Senior Colonel said, "We know, because your methods were
                                        4


      known to us."   My men were told to count three and shoot, because

      it takes your adversary three seconds to get his gun out.      On the
      fourth, you're dead!

            My raids and arrests in Rome were all carefully planned and

       nothing was left to chance.    For example, -when we arrested . two

       gestapo Colonels all contingencies were considered.      My Italian
       customs agents were sent out a week ahead of time and conducted a

       twenty four hour surveillance, this revealed that they kept a

       typical German methodical schedule.     If one of the Gestapo

       Colonels went out, (recognized by his walk), he was followed.

       The Italian police checked all apartments on the 2nd floor of all

       buildings one block on either side of the door from which the two

       Germans would exit.   Their gait was clocked on how long it would

       take them to walk from A to B, or A to C or A to D.      We knew when

       they went out for food, etc.    The morning of the raid all

r21    streets, three blocks on all sides of the building were sealed

       off by Italian police.    Cars would occasionally criss—cross the

       street and some strollers would walk by.    (My G.I.C. men with

       some dressed in women's clothing.     The cars contained armed C. I.

       C. and Italian police of the lightning squad.)     This was
7
       necessary to halt suspicion in case they were watching.       All

       second floor windows had an armed man ready.    Italian police

       notified all inhabitants that they would remain in their

       apartments until further notice.     The trap was set.   If by chance

      "they changed their schedule; then Plan II, a raid on the

       apartment was contemplated.    I was present to give the order.

          The front entrance was generally blown in with explosives.
          If the occupants didn't surrender within two minutes; then
          simultaneously, all entrances and windows were blown as well
          as any skylights or roof entrances. This was accomplished
          by the custom agents who had scaled the walls. Grenades
          were thrown into the building along with tear gas canisters.
          The customs agents with my C. I. C. charged with automatic
          weapons l'firing at anything that moved. We then would load
          all the weapons on waiting trucks and destroy what we didn't
          move.

           .SPA VIASIoil
      became the Italian internal situation, ,which at times had to be

     'nipped in the bud, before it could grow to endanger our security.

               The Christian democrats were the largest party, but had to

      rule with a coalition, with smaller parties.         The communist

     .boasted of the largest communist party in the west.          The left

     . wing scicialist with the communist were always a threat which


        !I
        I .


                                                                                   C
00000000000000
                                  Ti
                                              could spell disaster for Italy.    Over forty newspapers

                                              especially the communist and socialist inflamed the situation

                                              with irresponsible reporting.

                                                   During this period the head of the Iberian airways offered

                                              me a private plane and pilots to take me to Spain, at the

                                              invitation of Franco's government, to obtain the information on

                                              Petro Nenni's criminal record, while he fought with the

                                              communists during the Spanish Civil war.     Nenni was the head of


  00000000000000000000000000000
                                              the left wing socialist party, but we had him tabbed as a soviet

                                              agent. I had to refuse, but later I did receive the information

                                              and it was made public.    This hurt the communist left wing

                                              socialist coal?tion.

                                                   Also security was threatened by the influx of NKVD (now

                                              KGB), agent provocateurs.    The Vatican was in my area and I had

                                              to keep a watchful eye, since they were a communist target.       This

                                              surveillance took much of my time.     The refugee problem was
                                  1.1
                                              always there and kept increasing as an irritant and had to be

                                              watched closely.    Neo—fascist groups kept sputtering, but were no

                                              more than a nuisance, one of my most disturbing problems were the

                                              numerous and almost constant demonstrations.    My men and the

                                              Italian police had to be constantly alert that American soldiers

                                              and other allied'personnel on leave didn't accidentally get mixed

                                              up in these often explosive situations.    I was a help to the
                                        1,1

                                              Italian police.    My underground usually notified me on how to

                                              identify the leaders and agitators.    For example, often. the

                                              demonstrators wore black "T" shirts.    The leaders would wear a

                                              paper clip on the left side of the neck collar strip.      Prefect

                                              Moro's riot "Celere" squad would seek out these leaders and make
                                                                                        Cig


      short work of them.

              I was fortunate, my record of   Ito II
                                                       GI's hurt, remained
      intact.    Luckily, even curious American or allied soldiers were

      never injured in any of these riots.                                               ()

INSURANCE


     With all the enemies I had made, I decided that I needed

some very good life insurance.    I sent for the communist partisan

chief, a ruthless killer.    He didn't come, so I sent word that if

he didn't come on his own two feet, I would have him dragged in.

(Count Armenise, a wealthy Italian, who even owned Mica Mines,

had asked me to meet him on the outskirts of Rome.     He told me

that I was higher on the communist hit list than he was.        I told

him, "Good, before I leave Rome, I hope to be number one."        By

the tone of the newspaper article that appeared in Unita, in

April 1947, I must have been number one.

     The communist partisan came to my office.     I told him to sit

down in a chair close to the front of my desk.     I spoke harshly,

"I hear through the grapevine that you are following my actions,

and possibly intend to harm me and some of my men!"

     He blurted out, "Per carita, sei un amico nostro."    (Mercy

sakes no, you are •a friend of ours.")

     I told him, "(Bugardo) Liar, stop right there.     I am an

enemy, not a friend of yours."   I had a photograph of my two year

old son, whom I had never seen, on my desk.    I turned the picture

so he could see it, and said, "See the boy?    I want a better life

tor him.    You advocate a worse life."   I continued, "Don't

trouble to look for me, I'll let you know my itinerary anytime

that you want it!"

    At this point I took a pistol out of my drawer.     It was an

ugly looking six shooter.   It had a hair trigger and had killed a
 man.    I leaned over my desk and placed the front of the barrel

 against his forehead.    I told him, "Don't move.    This pistol has

 a hair trigger, and you can see it is loaded.      I would think less

of killing you' than a dog."

        His face turned white.   I could see his mind.   "The stories

of the unstable American with the eyes of the devil are true."

        I put the pistol on my desk and shoved a list of twelve

names in front of him and said, "If you take a shot at me and

misd, I will personally get the twelve of you.       If you kill me, I

have given orders that the twelve of you will be found in the

Tiber River floating face down.     This was the favorite trick of

the Borgias.     Note the list; you are Number 4 on it.    Take it

with you .     IF I LIVE --- YOU LIVE."

        Years later Willard Edwards, Washington Bureau Chief of the

Chicago Tribune, in an interview asked, "Did you really mark

twelve men for death?"     My answer was, "They surely must have

thought so, because I am still here."

        At this point I must ask%bh4gang   ecr, "Would I have killed

the twelve men?"     I used to reflect after such encounters, tees I

:becomtng a predator, as bad as the men I was tracking down and
                                                             ir
 confronting? Decorations, medals, citations meant little. lin

   the thrill of the hunt and the pounce for the kill that kept

me going?    Was this my natural make-up and could I control it in

the future?     I used to shrug it off and say to myself, "Time will

tell."

                              CHAPTER IV


                           THE ITALIAN MEDIA


     During one period in Rome, there were forty seven

newspapers, and more were springing up like mushrooms.      Much of

the reporting ranged from irresponsible to downright lies.      In

many cases, it endangered our security and that of our troops

still fighting the German armies in northern Italy.      I remembered

the words of the Chief of Police of Italy, "If you plan to apply

your American principles of fair play, then you better go home

now!"

     Our Military Government (AMG), let them run wild under the

guise of freedom of the press.    One day a story appeared in one

of the newspapers entitled, "The American dollars stained in

blood."   The obvious lies and distortion were totally

irresponsible.    I got angry and said to myself, "Freedom of the

press is not this," so I sent for the editor and decided we were

no longer going to be used as "whipping boys."   Enough was

enough.

    The editor came to my office, and I showed him the article.

He nonchalantly said, "Well, you know how it is, one

ahticle ...."

    I stopped him and said "Liar,"     the Italian word "Bugardo"

is much more expressive.    I continued, "One article, you insult

me to my face!"   I opened up a file in front of me.   It had at

least fifty similar articles from his newspaper.   I picked them
up and threw them in his face, and called him "face of bronze,"

an Italian expression which meant you can lie and keep an

expressionless face as if made of bronze.

     I got up in my chair and said in a loud voice, "Get out of

my office, your face pains me!"     He turned white, since I am sure

he heard of the crazy American.     He missed the door in his haste

and hit the side.    I picked up his hat on my desk and yelled,

"Take this with you,"    and threw it in his direction.   Crude but

effective.    The next day a long editorial appeared praising the

United States.

     I took the article to my Commanding General, General Dasher.

He looked up and said, "Did you have anything to do with this?"

I answered, "General, I wouldn't intimidate the press." I got no

answer except the half-smiling look he often gave me which,

without words, said, "I'll bet you didn't."

     My next encounter was with Unita, the communist party so-

called "free and democratic press," which our military government

authorities insisted that we allow to function freely.     I will

not bore the reader with the *diatribes they constantly printed

against the United States.     I awaited an opening to go after

their editor, who was the brother-in-law of Palmiro Toglatti t the

communist party chief and the present Minister of Grace and

Justice.    My informant in the Italian communist party advised me

that they were preparing an attack on me which would be printed

on. the front page of Unita.

     I sent for the communist editor and had him escorted to my

office.    I told him that I heard he was going to publish an

attack on me.    He, of course, denied it.
           I then said, "Go ahead, publish the article; and if there is

      one word in it that I don't like, and I am sure there will be

      many, I will arrest you, and see if your brother-in-law can get

      you out of my prison.   fl

      twill then come down to the prison with the article, stuff it in

      your mouth, make you eat it and digest it.    The article was

      printed three years later, in April 1947, six weeks after I had

      left Italy.   The two main points they made were that I suppressed

      the free and democratic press, and that I was an American

      terrorist.

           At this stage I decided that the putting out fires approach

      was not always effective.    I had to move into the media in a big

      way to counter the socialist and communist propaganda barrage fed
4'.
      mostly through the pretense of freedom of the press.

           Now I will digress briefly from the Italian media to the

      United States Army controlled "Stars and Stripes."    The

      editorials for some time were pure and simple communist

      propaganda.   In a check with the FBI, I found that the editor

      in - charge was a major and card carrying member of the United

      States Communist Party who had been assigned to the position

      directly from the United States.   The Theater Commander wanted to

      get rid of him.   I.urged him to wait since the scrubwoman at the

      "Stars and Stripes" building was my paid informant.    I was

      preparing a case against him and his First Sergeant for theft of

      United States Government property (selling newsprint on the black

      market.)   All I needed were affidavits from the buyers.    I told

      the General a criminal charge and trial by court martial would

      strip him of political support.
                                                                        6.1=

     In the meantime, since I had never seen my little son; I was

allowed to come home on 90 days Temporary Duty (TDY).      During my

absence, the major was relieved.    A political uproar followed.

Upon my return I asked General Dasher, why, General Lee had taken

the action?    He said, "You were gone, and I couldn't hold off

General Lee with you absent." I did finish the case, and the

criminal was sent to Washington.    The furor died down.

     One of my Italian friends had introduced me to a man whose

conversations left a lasting impression on me.     He was small in

stature and must have been severely burned on his face and arms.

The skin was stretched taut and a crimson color.    He was hard to

look at, but I never let on that I noticed his infirmity.      I sent

for him to come to my office.    My investigation revealed that he

had been the Chief of Reuters and Stefani News Agency.     In Europe

before the war, they ranked with our AP and UPI News Wire

services.

     Schuster came to my office and I asked, "How would you like

to go to work?"    He said, "But, Senor Filippo, (my first name in

Italian), I can't hold a gun, let alone use one."

     "I don't need you to use a gun.   I have men who are experts

in that field."    I continued, "Why don't you start a newspaper?"

     "But, Fillipo r it takes money; I can hardly support my

family at the moment."

     I came back with,   "I didn't ask you that.   Answer my

question -- do you want to start a newspaper?"

     Almost in disbelief, he said, "Yes, you are offering me back

my life.    You are serious, are you not?"

    Schuster, I don't joke about such matters.     However, before
                                                                         ts


        you answer further, listen to me.      If I finance you, I do. not        C)
                                                                                  C)
        intend to exercise editorial control.      I would be foolish even to
                                                                                  0
        attempt it, since this is not my line of business.      Amateurs do
                                                                                  0
        not advise professionals.     You are the best among professionals        0
        in you business.    Print the truth.    I will exercise no control,       0
                                                                                  0
        and I am sure   you will.show up most of these rags in Rome which
                                                                                  0
        call themselves newspapers."
                                                                                  0
             He took my hand and said, "You have given me the breath of           ()

        life.   I will never forget this, nor will my children."                  0

             "Schuster, stay in character.     You are an intelligent,

ti      strong — willed man.    Now stop the sentiment and get to work        I
                                                                                  C)

        don't want to waste my money and time." . (The reader must realize        0

        that in the much more expressive Italian language, such words

        sound different and carry a more meaningful connotation.)

             I gave him 3,500,000 lire (about $1,000.00) which,

        ironically, my men had taken frtuff the communists in an arms raid.
317..


        I .didn't have to use my confidential funds.     I told him, "This is

        to get started..   I will get you some equipment and more money as

        you need it, but don't go overboard."

             Two weeks later he. walked in with the first edition.     It was

        a tabloid, and distribution was to all Italian Senators,

        Legislators, key government officials and civic leaders,

        including ,leaders of industry:

             The articles on freedom, dignity of man y government,

        caPitalisid versus marxism, socialism, and even the United States

        Constitution were literary gems..   The man, without a doubt, was a

        genius in hid field..
                        •
             Within Tour Mouths; he waiKedtin my office and told me he

                                  •
                                                                                 671


          was self - sufficient and would need no more money and even
     -r   offered to pay back the seed money.    I refused and told him his

          writings were paying me back every day, with interest.

                  Right after the first advance, I had to inform my Commanding

          General -- General Dasher.    I.kept my fingers crossed.   I

          translated some of the articles which were beautifully written in

          a flowing, almost lyrical Italian.

               General Dasher, a very astute man who luckily thought very

          highly of me, said, "This is a new newspaper."    I answered, "Yes,

          Sir."

               He took the paper and said, "Now I have seen everything.

          Instead of intimidating editors, you start your own paper."

              I saluted and left his office.     He was looking at the paper

          and shaking his head.    I detected a slight smile and breathed a

          sigh of relief as I closed the door.

                Schuster's news tabloid continued to grow and prosper.       In    C)
                                                                             C)
           one action, he repaid my investment in him a thousand times over. c)

           He was one of the unsung heroes who saved Italy from Soviet             C)
           subversion.   I had to enter the United States Army Hospital,           C)

           because my skin was getting a scarlet tint.    The Army doctors         C)
                                                                                   C)
          feared Scarlet Fever and put me in the hospital for observation.
                                                                                   C)
           On the second day, one of my C.I.C. officers came to see me and         C)
           had Schuster in tow.    He told me, "It's a good thing I came           C)
                                                                                   C)
'11        along; this crazy little man was getting ready to fight the MP's
                                                                                   0
           because they wouldn't let him in to see you."
                                                                                   C)
                As they left, he asked me to call him, since it was of             C)
           utmost importance.    I only stayed a few days and, upon my             C)
                                                                                   C)
           release, I called, and Sbhuster asked me to come to his home.
                                                                                   ()
                When.I entered the sitting room, a large hulk of a man arose
                                                                                   ()
           from his chair.   He must have been 6,6" or 7' tall and must have

           weighed 300 pounds.    He spoke in a shrill voice or falsetto.     He   (-)

           offered me his hand and was introduced ae the Prince of former

           Royal House of Yugoslavia.    There were five other men present.

          They were introduced to me in turn, and each gave me a slight nod

          of respect as we shook hands.     I could see from the worn

          i.condition of their clothes that they had seen better days.

                A story began to evolve as if out of a movie, and I was one

      ;   of the lesser characters.     The Prince in his high voice began a

          tale of his family and Yugoslavia and the Balkans, in their days

          of glory before the Nazis and communism.    He Was obviously the
     leader.   He said he was proud to meet me, a member of the great

     army that defeated the Nazis and the individual who had stopped

     the communists in every move in Rome.    Finally, he got to the

     point.    They were going to build an underground which would

0    operate in every Balkan country occupied by the communists,

     hoping to lead to eventual liberation.

          At this point I asked, "What do you want of me?"

         The Prince continued, "We need a secure base of operation.

    We have selected Rome.    We know how you operate, and know we can

    operate from Rome only with your consent.      Schuster has assured

     us that you can be trusted, and your word is your bond."

         "Your goals are the same as mine," I revealed.      "I will let

     you operate in Rome.   In addition, I will protect you against any

    communist action, since they know I will strike at them hard if

    they attempt any covert operation in my area."

         The Prince said, "Thank you, friend; Schuster's description

    of you and your reaction was as he stated.     But, Sir, we are not

    men that just take, without giving something in return."      He

    motioned to one of the five;' he came forward and handed me a

    package.   He introduced himself as the former Minister of

    Interior of Yugoslavia.   He opened the package, and it

    contained --

         THE ENTIRE DETAILED OPERATIONAL NET OF THE SOVIET NKVD IN

    THE BALKANS.   SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS AND DETAILS OF THEIR OPERATION

    'INTO ITALY AIMED AT THE ITALIAN GOVERNMENT.    PHOTOGRAPHS OF THE

    NKVD CHIEF OF THE BALKANS AND INDIVIDUAL PHOTOGRAPHS OF HIS

    STAFF, AND THEIR MAIN OPERATIONAL HEADQUARTERS IN CZECHOSLOVAKIA

    AIMED AT THE WESTERN DEMOCRACIES.


0
     Any intelligence officer would recognize the tremendous

value of this bonanza immediately.      Not only were there names and
duty assignments, but alsb with the photographs we had the means

of quickly identifying each individual whether we needed others

to tail them and learn their present activities or to arrest them

for further questioning.    I had just been given the key to the

whole Soviet secret police in the new Soviet Empire Moscow was

establishing in Eastern Europe: Czechoslovakia, Hungary, Rumania,

Bulgaria, Yugoslavia and Albania.

     I called my C.I.C. and ordered them to send two cars of

armed agents.to escort me.    I was taking no chances of these

documents falling into the wrong hands.
                            AwAief
     g                1 leitetaiwartb:creteatteits,n and memorized every

detail.   I burned all- the papers except the photographs.      Since      C)

the details were engraved only in my brain, none of the                    C)
                                                                           C)
information, even future data, were ever compromised; nor were
                                                                           C)
the Prince's brave men, operating right under the nose of the              C)
NKVD, ever picked up and shot.                                             0
                                                                           C)
     My instinct, or some power greater than mine, drove me to
                                                                           C)
this action.   Later, as a member of the National Security                 C)
Council, I found out that my instinct was correct.       If I had          0

transmitted those documents to Washington, the Prince and his men
                                                                           0
Would have been quickly Wiped out.

                     As the war neared its end and the German armies were on the

      verge of defeat, the communists intensified their efforts to

      disrupt the Italian Government.                                             Their target was the government,
      and they stayed away from our installations.                                                                 NKVD agent
      agitators kept coming.                                     I picked them up as they arrived, and

                                              Santoro's Secret Police gathered in their contacts.

                                                   We were doing a good job.     I gave the Italian Police tear

                                              friend of mine, used the jeeps like a squad of soldiers -- jeeps

                                              orders from Washington that I would not arrest NKVD agents since

                                              we were not at war with Russia and the NKVD were not to be

                                              to be in Rome.   The General had the hardened look of the tough

                                              Airborne Combat Commander that he was.     He handed me the telegram

                                              and gave me no orders.     We did not speak a word.   I took the

                                              telegram, saluted and left.    When I got back to my office, I tore

                                              it up and threw it in the wastebasket.

ALLIED CONTROL COMMISSION


     My relations with British Military Intelligence were always

excellent.   I was trained in England by British MI-19, and made            0
many close comrades during the fighting at Salerno, Cassino and

Anzio.   I want the reader to note that I refer to British

Military Intelligence as a combat arm.      The guards at my prison

were tough, hard, reliable soldiers.
                                                                            0
     James Angelton, C.I.A., and I Were to meet and cross swords

several times during the balance of my military career.           Soon

after James Angleton's meeting with the.British Foreign Office

visitors,(among them Kim Philby) I noticed a change in my
                                                                            -0
relationship with the British and discovered control of most
                                                                            0
intelligence had reverted away from the War Office to the Foreign           C)

Office, and so was no longer vested in the military.                 0

     Brigadier Edmund Lowe, a fine British officer and gentleman,

w-dr=-hisize Deputy Commander of Rome Area Command!     We was a Veteran
of many battles and had been wounded in his right elbow.         He held

his pipe at a strange angle; and when he began to puff hard, I

would stay quiet.    My day-to-day operations were with him.       Being

d.former intelligence officer, fortunately he took me under his

wing as a student.    We became close friends..       I used to accompany

him on his morning walks.     Once I asked the old Scotsman,'.

"Brigadier, there is a saying that true Scotsmen never wear

anything under their kilts."     He answered, "Don't believe it,
00

00000000000000000000000000000000000000000
                                            :11   Son; it can get awfully cold down there!"

                                                       An event transpired which clearly reveals how fortunate I

                                                  was to have such a man over me in the chain of command.      One day

                                                  he sent for me.    There were two officers sitting in his office,

                                                  one a British Brigadier and the other an American Brigadier

                                                  General, both from the Allied Control Commission.   As I walked

                                                  in, the United States Brigadier General pointed to me and said,

                                                  "Get rid of him!"

                                                       Brigadier Lowe spoke calmly, "Why, and on what grounds?"

                                                       They began:


                                                       01.   "He is interfering with our control of the Italian
                                                             Government. He wrote a critical report in much detail
                                                             outlining that we were hindering instead of helping.

                                                             Italian Police. Again in much detail, he says we did
                                                             nothing to reestablish the forces of law and order. He
                                                             has interfered by training them and even supplies arms

                                                             he claims we did nothing to help them.

                                                             him.

                                                       #6.   He has suppressed free and democratic growth of
                                                             political parties.

                                                       #7.   He has even interfered with freedom of the press."

     The next morning he called me into his, office and handed me

a dispatch he was sending to the War Office.    It began by

addressing the two officer's from Allied Control Commission:

     "Gentlemen, since I am not in the habit of letting an event

such as transpired in my office yesterday be forgotten, I had my

secretary take down the proceedings.    I am sending you each a

copy of the dispatch I have sent to the War Office."

     On the way out, as we were going down the corridor, I said,

"Brigadier, it seems as if you are always pulling me out of

trouble."     His answer was typical, "Son, we can handle trouble;

as long as everyone is sniping at you, you are doing a good job.

What I couldn't handle is you doing nothing; then I will get rid

of you."

     A sequel to this story happened a few months later.      This

time, I had expected it and was ready for the attack.     The

Italians had been warning that British Embassy personnel had been

asking questions on my relationship with Italian Fascist Generals

and other high-ranking Fascist officials.

     It appeared to me that these inquiries began soon after the

Foreign Office personnel visit to Jim Angleton, the C.I.G. Chief.

I gave the communist angle little thought; because, although Kim

Philby was one of .them, I didn't know of his communist

connection.    He must have known I had Created havoc with

cb.mmunist plans in Italy.

     During this period, I began more and more to hear the words

"White socialist."    This term was a puzzle to me, since I had not

grown up in thp university atmosphere that had spawned the

American socialist's including the American Red socialist
•
t
                                                                                Ca:H
                                                                                 C
                                                                                 ()
          thinkers of my generation.   Italy had various hues of socialists,

          from Pietro Nennifs extreme left pro-Communist Socialist Party,

          to Saragatts right wing Socialist.   Saragat had been recommended

          to me as an honest, decent politician worth talking to.       I

          arranged a conference, and received my first lesson on socialism

          from an expert.   His conclusion:   Elements in the British Foreign

          Office, together with the United States Embassy, are promoting a

          socialist form of government for Italy.     To make it more

          palatable, they call it "White" socialism.    They are promoting

          that socialism is closer to communism; and, therefore, they

          understand better how to counter them. In Rome, he said, "You are

          in the way.   Be careful!"   Now I understand the rumors that some

          serious actions were planned against me and some of my men.

                   The case against me was being compiled by an ex-Major
    Sri
          in the British Embassy, now a civilian Foreign Service Officer.

          The plan was simple but effective.   The, charge went and was

          passed to make it appear as high-level information:

                         Foreign Office

                              to

                          British Theater Commander

                             to

                          United States Theater Commander

                              to

                         United States G-2 at Allied Force Headquarters


                          Note:    The United States G-2 at AFHQ had just
                                   taken over from Brigadier General Smith,
                                   with whom I was on the best of terms.
             This was the best time to strike a new General, who didn't

        know me and knew little about operations in Rome.     General

        Dasher called me to his office, and Brigadier General Howard was

        sitting there.   He said, "The British have accused you of

        releasing a Fascist General and want you relieved."

             I answered, "General Howard, I have arrested and released

        many Fascists, and among them military officers, including

        Generals.   I have written copies .of all my arrests and releases.

        The Fascists are no longer a threat and have not been for years.

        Except for some small neofascist groups, who are no more than a

        nuisance, they are gone for good.   This is a poor excuse on why

        the British want me out.   Furthermore, I resent the accusations."
t; .
             At this point, General Dasher hit his fist on the table in

        anger and shouted, "If they make any accusations against my G-2,

        make them prove it."

             General Howard was startled.   In a week he called back and

        said, "Forget it."

             Now it was my turn. I went to the British Embassy and

        confronted the ex-Major.   I threw a bunch of sworn statements on

        his desk.   "There is a statement from your ex-chauffeur and every

        black marketeer he sold British supplies to, which you stole; and

        there is a compilation of how much motley you made.   Now, go ahead

       'and compile another phony report on me."

             At this point, I decided to take out another insurance

        policy.   I walked down the hall to the ex-Major's boss.   A guard

        was at his door; I pushed him out of the way and walked in.     The

        Foreign Office civilian arose and said, "I have no business with
                                                                                                            ()
                                                                                                            ()
     you!"     I answered, "That's right, but let me give you some good                                     c)
     advice.      If any of my men fall off the top of a building, you and

     some of yours will fall off.                  Leave mine alone, and I will leave 8
     yours alone."
                                                                                                            0
           I turned and walked out.                  A truce must have been declared.
                                                                                                            C)
     We never had another problem.                   A socialist Italy had to wait.                         C)
