7)                                        AND

                                 THE CASSINO AT LUGANO


               My commanding officers were the best.     I was most fortunate

          in that they always supported me, through the nature of my

          operation was such that I could not clear every action with them

          In advance.   But I always felt assured that they would stand

          solidly with me, which they did.   This was one of the main

          factors that assured my success.   My protective security screen

          also encompassed them, because my job description read "All

          Allied Personnel."

               I took a trip to Allied Foree Headquarter (AFHQ) in Caserta,

          Italy, to see General Smith, Assistant Chief Of Staff, G-2, of

          Allied Force Headquarters.   He was a fine gentleman and a good

          officer.

               I had never been to his office.      When I announced my name, I

          was surprised that the entire staff, officers and enlisted came

          up to shake hands.   They said, "So many reports, all the phone

          calls, but we,have never met you."      I felt good about this

          reception, and figured I must be doing something right.
                                                                                ;vc
            I was ushered into General Smith's office.     I had asked for

      the meeting, because of a matter of some importance concerning

      the General and his Chief of Staff.    I began, "A few weeks ago,

      both of you were invited to the casino at Lugano (the casino is

      Italian territory completely surrounded by Swiss territory.)        You
      were invited by two civilians, one an American and the other an

      Englishman, who passed themselves off as representatives of the

      Allied Command in Germany.   They gave you chips, and you gambled

      at the tables.    I even know that you didn't win.   The two

      civilians are crooks, and one is an ex C.I.C. and the other an

      ex FSC.   They,muscled their way into managing the casino as

      Allied representatives and are raking in about $3,000.00 a day.

      They used you as evidence of their validity.     I don't know if you

      know it General, but all dealers and croupiers are Italian police

      or Italian intelligence officers.    Their Chiefs in Rome report to

      me.   Casinos are a great source of intelligence.    Their financial

      and biographical files are invaluable.   I came here mainly, since

      the casino is out of Rome jurisdiction, to ask your permission to
7-1
      arrest those two con-men_thiftves, who used you.

            General Smith had a firm look on his face and said, "You

      have my permission as of now."

            I answered, ?General, may I use your phone?"    I called

      Colonel Gono Moreno, Chief of C.I.C., Rome, and told him to call

      Chief Ferrari and General De Pasquale of S.I.M., and tell them to

      arrest those crooks at Lugano and deliver them to me.     Then

      spread the word that the casino at Lugano has never been under

      Allied control.

            The two con-men were good at their game.     They must hate
         intercepted the arrest order or been informed by a paid worker.

         They never came back to Lugano, but stayed in Switzerland.     They

         also found out that the arrest order had come from my office.     A
         few days later, an Italian asked to see me.    He had a message

         from the two in Switzerland.    It was, "They are coming to Italy,
0
         first to the C.I.C. in Milan, then stop in Rome on their way to
0
0        Caserta (meaning General Smith)."    I told the Italian, "I am not
0        going to arrest you because I want you to take this message back.
0        Tell those two thieves to please come to Italy, and I invite them
0
         to Rome where I have a special cell waiting for them.     You! Your

0        face insults me!   If I ever see it again, I will lock you up and

         throw away the key.   Get out before I change my mind!"

              This, of course, was their bluff or the long shot proposal
0   71
         of some sort of deal with me.   The minds of such men always

         figure there is an angle or deal in the offering.   I also
0        surmised that in their smugness and the comforts of Switzerland,

         they must have also figured that they beat me.

              At the United States Embassy, there was an FBI agent, Ray

         Farrell, who later became United States Commissioner of

         Immigration.   When I was at the White House where we worked
0
         together a second time, I turned the report on the two characters
0        at Lugano over to the FBI. The British portion went to their

         authorities.   Six years and eleven months later, just before the

         expiration of the statute of limitations, and possibly their

         money gone, the I.R.S. would move in for tax evasion.

              All such cases were handled in. the same manner.   I couldn't

         detain or hold a person's money unless I could prove the source

0        of it.   For example, a soldier, formerly a New York lawyer, tried
                                                                            C)


    to buy money orders for $45,000.00, obviously made on the black

    market or in a crap game.    When I asked him where he got the

    money, his wise answer was, "You're investigating me, you find
1
    out."    Perhaps he wasn't so wise; six years and eleven months

    later, he was nailed.

         The sequel to the "Lugano Casino" was an interesting one.      A
    few weeks after the end of the "Lugano" case, two men showed up

    in my office.   One was Italy's top lawyer, and the other a member

    of the Swiss legislature.   They were the legal owners of the

    Casino.   They came expressly to thank me and invited me as a
                                                                            a
    guest of theirs at the casino.   I told them it was not wise for

    me to be seen at the casino in light of what had a happened.

    They then asked me what they could do for me.   I told them

    nothing; it was my job to do what I had done.

         They continued, "But in some manner we would like to show

    our appreciation."   I answered, "Well, you can.   Your casino is

    an excellent source of intelligence, perhaps sometime in the

    future you can reciprocate with information I may need for my

    official duties."

         Their answer was, "At any time that you call, you will have

    free access to our files.   On that, you have our word and our

    hand."

         I said, "Good, if anyone calls, in my name, their

    authenticity will be verified by a brief resume' of what was

    spoken and transpired at this meeting.    Only the three of us will

    know, and I will put nothing on paper."

         We shook hands, and the meeting ended with thanks from both

    sides.    A valuable link in my informant net, if needed.

                             MUSSOLINI'S TREASURE
0
0
0         During the final weeks and days of the war in April and May,
0    1945, as the German armies were fleeing north in disarray, we
0
     kept receiving reports of the impending surrender of all the

     German armed forces.   I began to brace my counterintelligence
0    units for an influx of Gestapo and SS fleeing the scene of their

     crimes.    I warned my men that these were dangerous, deadly

     killers.    They would fight and kill to resist capture.    I

0    reminded them, no bravado tactics, only a simple warning with
0    drawn guns and they had three seconds to get their hands up in

     the air.    On a lower level, I told them many stray German and
0
     Italian soldiers would be streaming into Rome.     I only wanted

     them rounded up.   Fascist officials also would be coming to Rome,
0    and I wanted only the fanatics held.      Soon after the surrender of
0
     the Axis armies, a Fascist official told us that Benito Mussolini

0    and his entourage would try to get to Switzerland.     In an armored
0    car he had a fortune in currency, jewels, gold and silver

     bullion, and coins.

0         I immediately dispatched armed counterintelligence agents
0   iand alerted our forces near the area.     About a week later, I

    'received a report that communist partisans had ambushed the

    Mussolini convoy heading for the Swiss border, killed the escort

     and had taken Mussolini and his mistress captive.

          They had, obviously, taken the treasure that the Dictator

0
                                                                               2;:rf   C)
                                                                                       0

       had with him.    (Estimates were that it amounted between                       0
                                                                                       C)
       $300,000,000 and $500,000,000.)    Much of this estimate was based
                                                                                       0
       on gold taken from the Bank of Italy in Rome.                                   0
            During this period, I had occasion to visit Northern Italy.                C)
                                                                                       C)
       While I was there, I thought that I would travel around and ask
                                                                                       0
       some questions.    I stopped in the little village of Bardello in
                                                                                       ()
       the province of Varese.    The village was between Lago di Varese               0
       and Maggiore, and near Funte Teresa, Switzerland.        All the
                                                                                       ()
       ingredients were right, lakes and near the Swiss border.       I had
                                                                                       0
       received information that the treasure has been hidden in a lake

       to safeguard it from armed Allied patrols, such as mine.       I saw a

       small boy of about 10 years.   I called him and asked him if he                 0
                                                                                       0
       knew a good trattoria where we could get some good Italian food.
                                                                                       0
       He asked, "Senor, you are an American?' I. answered, "Yes, I am an              C)

       American Arty Officer."    He said, "I want to go to America                    C)
                                                                                       C)
S1     someday."   I said, "Good, now where is the trattoria?"      He
                                                                                       C)
       answered, "I will show you."   I replied, "Good, jump in, ride                  C)
4:1    with me, stay with us; and I will buy you a gelato."                            C)
                                                                                       C)
            He sat with us and during the conversation, I asked, "Have
                                                                                       C)
       you seen any partisans near the:lakes?"    He said, "No, Senor, but
                                                                                       C)
       my 'father saw them throw something in Lake Como two weeks ago;                 C)
       then they came back and .took it out.   It was very heavy.     The

       boat had to have a crane on it." .

            "Can you show me the place?" "Lake Como is not far."          He

      'said, "I will have to ask my Father."* We received permission, and

       he took us to the spot.    Natives in the area confirmed his

      informatiOn.     I offered to pay him, but he wanted only one of my

       United States pins from my blouse and one of the insignia of rank


                                                       si   '
                                                                                    ;-

           from my shoulder straps.

0               I asked him his name.    He said, "Roger Carvello."   Thirty
0          years later I met this same boy, now a grown _man, in Naples,
0          Florida.    We are still good friends.   He remembered the United
0
0        . States Army Officer; but not until 1974, did he know that I was

           looking for Italy's stolen treasure.

                I discussed the loss of this treasure often with the Chief

           of Police of Italy.    We tried .to trace it, but found out that the

           bulk of it went into the Communist Party coffers.    The remainder

           was taken by the sons of the proletariat, who ceased to be
0          workers and joined the hated capitalistic classes.     Their
0
           idealogy ran thin when riches were involved.     Over the next few
0
0          years, I often received information on these culprits. I turned
0          this intelligence over to the Italian Police.    I used to joke

           with the Chief of Police of Italy.    I used to say, "Excellency,
0
0         let's waylay the Soviet couriers when they bring gold bullion to

          finance the Communist Party and get some of your gold back."         He

           would laugh and say "Perhaps." (The Russians, during the Spanish

          Revolution, took all of Spain's gold to Moscow.)

               The communist partisans killed all of Mussolini's escorts

          and hung him and his mistress by the heels from meat hooks in the

          city square.    This atrocity had two purposes:   One, to kill all

          witnesses; the second, to show any informants by a shocking

          example, that the meat hook would be waiting for them if they

         'talked.     This episode of Mussolini's treasure and death was a

          good example of the duplicity and true character of most of the

          Italian communists.

               I wrote a full report on Mussolini's treasure for the United


    .1                                      •
Lri
      States Army.   In 1948, a lengthy news story appeared in a Rome

      newspaper.   It was nothing more than an elaboration of the

      details I touched upon in the preceding pages.   Since I had the

      best and most detailed information on the disappearance of the

      treasure, the Italian Government accepted my version.   It would

      have been a feather in my cap to have returned this treasure to

      the Italian Government; but I was soundly defeated in this quest,

      and was left holding the proverbial empty bag.
                                          UMBERTO
LOU
          7                        CROWN PRINCE OF ITALY


                I received a phone call and the voice on the other end

           announced that he was the military aide, to Umberto, the Crown

           Prince.    Unberto came on the telephone and invited me to spend

           the weekend at his hunting lodge, not too far from Rome.      I

           accepted because this would be a new experience.    I immediately,

           went to the Deputy ComMander, because I knew that Brigadier Lowe

           was interested in the coming election where by the Italian people

           would choose between the Monarchy or a Republic.    He instructed

           me to feel out the Crown Prince and his followers on their

           intentions if the Monarchy lost the election.    Our position was

           neutral.

                I took two of my C.I.C. Captains, both of whom spoke fluent

          Italian and enjoyed hunting.    When we arrived we were met by six

           high ranking military officers and the Crown Prince himself. The

          lodge was like something that you see in the movies.     The

           preserve itself was beautiful and natural in appearance.      After

          the introductory niceties, we were served, a light lunch.      I sat

          at the Crown Prince's right and he thanked me for what I had done

          in Rome and he knew in detail how I had destroyed the communist

          f arms cache and stopped all talk of a revolution.   After lunch we

          Were given hunting gear and I looked comical in my alpine cap
      4

          with a feather in the band. I was given a twin barrel, Berretta

          shotgun and the head huntsman was assigned as my hunting partner.

          Later I could see why. Not only was he my guide, but if I missed


                               •
a bird, he was so good that he would bring his shotgun over his

shoulder, (it was slung on his back) and bring down the 'game that

I missed.   A dozen or so soldiers were with us.    Their job was to
retrieve the game and take it back to the lodge.     By the time we
returned to the lodge to freshen up, it was plucked, or dressed,

cooked and prepared for a dinner literally fit for a King..

     That night naturally, the conversation turned to politics

and the coming vote.     I told the Prince that' our orders were to

remain neutral, but I would see to it that there would be no

rioting or intimidation of voters in Rome and we would do our

best to attempt to keep the balloting honest.

     He was pleased with my approach and thanked me and said he

was sure that in the key city, Rome, there would be law and;

order.   Also, if the monarchy lost, he, in .the best interest of

his country, would leave Italy.

     The next day, a Boar hunt was planned,.    They gave me shells

loaded with pumpkin balls (a sold shot like a marble.)     We went

to an area, where paths through the woods led to water.      We had

me climb a tree, because the hunter had to be 10 feet above

ground, or the Boer could pickup the scent.     I sat in the tree

for hours and was not too pleased and uncomfortable.     I saw no

Boars, but the party got one.     Late that afternoon we had a

delicious Boar roast.

     I never saw the Crown Prince again.     He went into exile

'after the election.    But, I was awarded the decoration, "Knight

Officer of the Crown of Italy."

     I reported to Brigadier Lowe and he was pleased to hear that

the Crown Prince would leave Italy if he lost the election.       He


                  •
    Li                                                                     :9A4
          explained that if he remained it could cause riots and unrest.

          He instructed me to write a report which he would forward to

          London.

0             On my way to my office I thought, "I wish I could always

0         gather intelligent in such a pleasant and sumptuous manner.

      THE REPUBLIC OF ITALY

Ji
            The Italian Monarchy was on its way out, and the Republic of

      Italy was being established.     Before my wife end child arrived in

      Italy, General Dasher allowed me to go home on 90 - day temporary

      duty to visit with my three year old son, whom Iliad never seen.

      Upon my return, Italian officials brought me thousands of fake

      ballots and false registrations.     They said, "If you, had been

      here, this fraud would never have happened and the Monarchy would

     • not have lost.,"   I answered, "Yes, perhaps I would have stopped

      the fraud, but the gods work in strange ways.     Did they

      temporarily divert me so that I would not interfere with

      something that had to be?     I cannot change destiny.   Now I am

      back to insure that there will be no interference from the

      communists or anyone else with the foundation of the fledgling

      Italian Republic, which now is a fait accompli and wholly

      accepted."

           During my tour in Rome, I was awarded the Order of the Crown

      of Italy (Knight Officer) by Prince Umberto, Crown Prince       of the

      House of Savoy.     The decoration i4as presented to me at a ceremony

J     and banquet which *lasted all afternoon and into the night.       About

      30 high-ranking officers and other military officers, all friends

      oc mine, were present.     The Crown Prince's Military Aide, of

      general rank, placed the medal around my neck.     It was a

      beautiful medal of mother of pearl with a gold outline.       The food

      was abundant, the wine flowed freely and I was among friends.

           When I arrived at my hotel suite, still wearing the medal,


                            •
     feeling no pain and in a happy frame of mind, my wife, Eunice,

     wasn't impressed, especially with my condition.        I said, "I am

     now a knight, and I can go into battle wearing your colors flying

     from my helmet."    She answered. "If you fall on your face now, I

     doubt that you could get back on your Feet; and if you were

     wearing armor, I doubt if you would even be able to move, let

     alone get on a horse,    some knight you would make."

             But deep down she must have felt some pride.    A few nights

     later, we were going to a formal dance.    Eunice had flaming red

     hair.    She came out wearing a low - cut green evening gown, and

Et   around her neck she had a close*- fitting black ribbon and

     hanging from the ribbon was my medal, the Order of the Crown of

     Italy.    Even I was impressed.   A beautiful decorative choker.       It

     was shaped like a Maltese cross, each point joined by four signs
                                                           rfl,ftemys. !Le
     of infinity in gold, a gold band around it and the sign orASavoy

     in the center.

           THg_pRINCESS BORGHESE
                                                                                    a
                Over a few years time the bond between Senor Vigorelli and

           myself grew tighter.     He was a Partisan Chief and was a man of

           great courage.     I was attracted to such men like him and my

           courageous custom guards.    Perhaps it was a shortcoming in my

           makeup that drew my admiration.     During combat and in raids I was

           always afraid.     It was like a sickening feeling in my stomach,

           and a growing urge to panic.    But, once I got involved all

           feeling left me and I would function as a machine with no feeling

           and with conditioned automated moves.    Once the action was over,

           fear and panic would return and usually ended in intense
711        perspiration.    I often wished I could be•like them, they claimed

           I was, but I had misgivings.

                Vigorelli, also had another side.     He often showed

           compassion.     My favorite story on him fits the theme of my tale

           of individuals of character and courage.     My wife and son had
;IL

           arrived in Rome and we were living in a suite in the Ambassador

           Hotel in Rome.    One morning at 2 a.m., my phone rang.      The clerk

           said there is a lady and a gentleman here to see you.        I

           answered, "Put him on the phone."    It was Vigorelli. He began, "I

           am sorry to bother you at this time of the morning."      I stopped

           him and said, "No apologies, you come right up and are always

           welcome day or night."    When they arrived, he introduced me to a

           striking, stately woman, the Princess Borghese a close friend of

           his wife and family.    She began, "I an sorry to disturb you at

           this time of the morning, but I had to see you on a matter of


      V.
      utmost importance to me and my family."      I answered, "Senor

      Vigarelli, needs no appointment to see me day or night, if he

      thought this visit was necessary at this time of the morning, I

      respect his judgement, and I only hope I can help."       Vigarelli

      added, "It was necessary that the Princess was not seen with me

      or seen coming to see you.     You will understand why we have come

      unannounced at 2 a.m."

             The Princess then began, "My husband is Prince Borghese,

      former commander of the 10th Flotilla M.A.S.      He is wanted bA,the

      allies as a fascist and by the communists as a war criminal. I

      haven't heard from him since the war ended, and I fear for his

      safety.     If he falls in communist hands they will kill him.     I

      know of no other place to turn to, but you.     Senor Vigorelli said

      you will understand and can be trusted above all others.       Will

      you help me?     You have kept other Italians from falling into the

      wrong hands and, many are alive because of you.    If not for me,

     'but. for our children, help their Father."

             At this point I must fill in the background.     Just before

      the end of the war, the communist decided they could move in on a

      defenseless Italy.    They set their eyes on Trieste.    They wanted

      the city.    The German Army was on the verge of defeat and what

      Was   left of the, Italian Army was fighting with us at Cassino.

      The Yugoslav communist, on orders from Moscow, began to cross the
    P, border to a defenseless Trieste.   They were unexpectedly met by

      the 10th Flotilla M.A.S., a marine type unit under the command of

I    Prince Borghese.     There was a fierce fighting and the 10th

     Flotilla, was almost wiped out, but they stopped the communists.

      The war ended and General Alexander, Commander of all allied
                                                                          C)
                                                                          C)
                                                                          C)
  armies, issued an ultimatum to the communists.       "Don't march on    C)
 Trieste or my armies will march."       (The allied armies at this       C)

 point were at full strength.)                                            C)
                                     I went to General Pasquale, Chief
                                                                          C)
 of Italian Intelligence and made an inquiry of the whereabouts of        C)
 Prince Borchese. The General and his deputy, looked at each other        C)
 in surprise.       I realized they knew but couldn't talk.    I said,    C)
                                                                          C)
 "General, I am not here to arrest him, but to help if I can."
                                                                          C)
 They asked me to accompany them.       We went to the office of the      C)
 Chief of Staff of the Italian Army.       He said, "Corso, we trust      C)

 you and know what you have done for us.       We know in the case of     C)
                                                                          C)
 Prince BorgheSe we can count on you if we need help.         He is a
                                                                          C)
 hero, never has been a fascist and is now safe at the military           C)
 island prison protected by his colleagues."                               C)

      I was thinking of my response when I heard the Princess
                                                                           C)
 Borghese plead again, "Will you help me?"       I answered, "I think I
                                                                           C)
 know how you feel, in the next room is my wife, during the                C)

 fighting, at times she didn't know for months if I was dead or            C)
                                                                           C)
 alive.       I never saw' my boy until he was three years old.   You

 were brought here by my friend Vigorelli.       I would trust him with    C)
 my life. 'Because he brought you, he conveys this same trust to

 you, but what I have to say, for your husband's safety, must

 never leave this room. . You cannot tell your children or your

 family."

      She answered, "Yes, yes.". She took my hand as if to bind

 her word.       I then continued, "Your husband is well and safe, in

 Custody of and protected by his own people. - I played no part

• except offered my help if needed.      I hope some day to meet this

 brave man."


          •
           She began to sob and tightened her grip on my hand.   At this

      point, my sleepy three year old son must of escaped his Mother,
Pan
      and came in and jumped on my lap.   Eunice was in close pursuit.

      The Princess took the little boy from me and kissed him.   I

      introduced my wife to her, she knew Vigorelli.   As they were

      leaving, Vigorelli embraced me and said, "Thank you friend."    He

      then kissed EunEies' hand.
'11        The next day a dozen boxes arrived filled with toys for my

      little boy.   A simple card with no signature said, "Grazie."

     There was one minor irritant which was a concern to the

Command.     I received a call from General Dasher; and in his

office was a Captain in charge of our APO, or Military Post

Office.    The little APO, in one month, had sold ten times the

payroll of the Command in money orders.    The General asked me if

I knew how this was happening.

     I told him, "Yes, Sir, it is very simple.    Money orders

bought without a payee's name, are taken to the black market.

They are treated as dollar instruments.    The money orders bring

disproportionate amounts of lire or military script.     A G.I. can

easily double his money in a few days.    That is one channel.     The

second is:    Officers and United States civilians exchange them

for dollars at the American Express office.    These dollars then

go into the black market exchanged for large amounts of lire or
                                                                                P


          script."

               General Dasher asked, "Can we stop it?"

               "Yes, Sir. There is a theater directive that no Americans in
0
          Italy can have dollars in his possession.     I will stake out the

          outside of the American Express office and pick     up every
0   E     American, military and civilian, and deliver them to the Chief of
0
0         Staff; and he can prefer charges.     Or, send them to their units

0         for disciplinary action.   However, the money orders going into

          the black market are another problem.     They find their way into


0         the Vatican Bank.   My jurisdiction ends at the Vatican walls.

          How they find their way to the United States, or are cashed in, I


    Ti    don't know; but to make it harder, let's make each purchaser fill

          in the payee's name."

               General Dasher said, "Are you sure, Phil, about the

0         Vatican?"
0   5[1        "Yes, Sir.   I will send one of my men to the Vatican Bank to
0         purchase United States money orders.    Tomorrow I will put

0         $2,000.00 worth of our money orders on your desk."

               He an iswered, "Go ahead, Phil, so then I can tell the Theater
0         Commander I saw them with my own eyes.    I did put them on his

          desk, and he said, "I'll be damned.    Now what will you do with

          them?"   I told him, "Send my man back out and tell him to bring

          back my $2,000.00 and keep the profit."

               The General was laughing and shaking his head when I left.

          I thought, it's a good thing he trusts me.    The purchasing of

          money orders dropped just about to normal shortly thereafter.

               General Dasher called me on the phone an d said, "Phil, my
                                                                                   C)
                                                                                   C)
                                                                                   C)
          aide is bringing a distinguished visitor to your office.     Take
                                                                                   C)
          care of him, he needs some information.    When he walked in, I          C)
          recognized him.    He was Fiorello La Guardia, former Mayor of New       C)
                                                                                   C)
          York and now head of the relief agency in the United States
                                                                                   C)
          sending large quantities of food to Europe to help the needy.       He   C)
          sat down and began.   "Colonel, I have. gotten reports of abuse of

          our relief assistance especially in the distribution of food             C)
                                                                                   C)
          stuffs.   The General said you know about such practices."      I
                                                                                   C)
-11       answered, "Yes, Sir, I can give you some examples, but I am               C)

          afraid the news will not be good."                                        C)
                                                                                    C)
               I was told he was a remarkable man, his answer verified or
                                                                                    C)
          confirmed what I had heard.   He said, "As head of the agency I           C)
          must take responsibility for the errors and shortcomings of my            CD
          organization."    I answered him by saying, "You can't be too
                                                                                    CD
                                                                                    C)
          critical of yourself, since you are often placed in a situation,          C.
          in which you can do very little, in addition you can do very


      1   little as far as Washington is concerened.     Your biggest problem

          is with the communist and their friends and sympathizers in our

          Government.

              He then said, "I believe you.    Can you explain the details

          to me?"   And so E began, "I have found out from Embassy sources

          that upon orders from Washington the bulk of the aid has been

          allotted to European countries now under communist control and

                                                                                   '
                                                                       bultmenrs Tor example, of food such as flourj

                                       JJ        wheat etc., are arriving in Yugoslavia and the bags and boxes are


0 0 000000
                                                 restenciled in the native language, "From Mother Russia."      The

                                                 same is true in Hungary, Poland, Czechoslovakia, Rumania, etc.

                                       Li        The people do not know that the food is from the United States.

                                                 In Italy and in my area, Rome, there are about twenty

                                                 distribution centers.     The only way the people can get a ration

                                                 of food is to show a communist party card."

                                                         I could see the look of concern on his face when he said,

                                                 "What can be done?"     I answered, as for the communist countries,


 0000000000000000Q000000000000000000
                                                 that is up to you and perhaps the Department of State, whom I do

                                                . not trust.    All I can see is that you issue orders that if the

                                                 practice does not stop, you will cut off aid.     As for Rome, I

                                                 intend to stop the practice.     I have met with the Italian

                                                 authorities.    We are now collecting the names of those that gave

                                                 the order, that there will be no ration without a communist party

                                                 card.    Within 10 days, all centers will be closed and the

                                                 perpetrators will be arrested.    The centers. will reopen and food

                                                 will go to the needy.    There will be an announcement from the

                                                 Italian authorities' and similar actions will be taken throughout

                                                 Italy."

                                                      He was most pleased, I then said, Mr. Laguardia would you

                                                 like to visit some of the centers?"    His answer was, "Yes, I

                                                 would like to very much.
                                                                        .."

                                                      I said, "Sir, I won't go with you, because I am too well

                                                 known and may alert them, but if you don't mind I will send one

                                                 of my best men a former New York Detective and a custom Guard


                                            •     Lieutenant.. They are the best, and you can feel safe with them.

                                              They also know the situation very well."
                                            .:%
                                                   I shook hands with hih and he thanked me.        Later, I received

                                                 'a note of appreciation._
          uuring the early days of our occupation, Rome was overrun.

11   with "Scognizzi" a translation is urchins.     They were wild packs

     of boys that roamed the streets day and night.     On one occasion

     an Italian friend told me, he came to Rome and was overwhelmed by

     a pack of Scognizzi.   He said, he opened the door of his

     automobile and he was overwhelmed.    About 20 of them jammed in

     his car like ants.   He couldn't move his arms or legs.   They

     stripped the inside of his car of his luggage and everything not

     bolted down and then just as quickly disappeared.    They would

     come up to you in the street and offer to sell you almost

     anything.   It was not unusual for one to come to you and in good

     English say "Hey C. I.., want to sleep with my sister she is a
                                                                               0
     virgin, $1.0.00 extra."   I used to grab one and begin a torrent of

     words in Italian and say "Let's go td the police."    they would

     begin phoney pleas, of my Mother is sick and needs money, etc.        I

     would turn them loose with a boot in the rear.    As they were

     running I could hear choice curses aimed at me, that were not

     intended for tender ears.

          After a few months,. I noticed the Scognizzi began to

     disappear from the streets.   I made some inquiries of the police

     and after a few days a priest called on me.    He began a most

     interesting story.   His tale was a follows:

-


                                                                               c
         I had been trying for months to lure the Scognizzi, to camps
0
    and havens we set up outside of Rome.     I had to bribe' them to

0   have them come-     I would give them a meal, clean beds, clothes

    and even shoes.     But, as soon as they left, they or their

    families would sell the shoes and clothes on the black market and
0
0   they would reappear on the streets as ragged and dirty as ever.
0   We decided that the only solution was to keep them at the camps,

    permanently.

         One day while trying to lure a group with cigarettes, the

    police pulled a raid and I was arrested with the others and

    charged with dealing in cigarettes on the black market.      The raid
0
    had netted about 30 boys and one priest, all charged with black

    market activities.     When the Judge heard my story, he checked and
0   found that it was true.    I was surprised when he sentenced the
0
    entire group to 30 days at my camp in my custody.      From the group

0   in my custody I learned two things.     One, that Rome was divided
0   into territories.     Each territory had districts protected by the
0
    organization.     The districts were headed by a 16 year old, the

0   territory by a 17 year old and the area on top by a group of 10

    year olds.__ The lower level gangs ranged from 6 years_ old to 15

    years old.     The second revelation was that they trusted no one,

    even prie9ts:    Their attitude was no one gives you anything for

    nothing.     This applied to what we offered.   They said, "You have

    to have an angle, and you will begin to preach and give sermons

    to turn us into altar boys and priest.     Your holy attitude seems

    phoney to. us.    What has you God ever given us?"

         As you can see we had our job cut out for us.     I was most

    fortunate that in the group, in my custody, and without my
knowledge was a 16 year old District Chief.   He was an

intelligent boy, and made his own decision, that maybe Were was

something to this.   He went out and brought in two District

Chiefs.   Finally, a territory head came in to observe and stayed.

The Scognizzi (urchins) followed.   The Italian boys' town came

into being.

     The priest ended his story by saying, "The best thing that

ever happened to me was that I was arrested, charged and

sentences as a black marketeer.   God works in mysterious ways."

             w hysrpil    ofilIRCE/41-
0
0                   Churchill recommended an invasion of Italy and a thrust into
0             Yugoslavia, into what he called "The Soft Underbelly."     He was
0             overruled by the United States military high command.     In later
               years when I was on the NSC staff I often wondered.             Was
0             this a military decision or one influenced by political

              ideologist within our government?     In later years I discovered
    :-•
0             that military officers often went along with policy consideration
      LI      against their best interests, but which they accepted without a
0             murmur.     These situations puzzled me, but most often they
        Li
              disgusted me that men of proven courage would bow before a "so-
0   _
              called" civilian counterpart of questionable loyalty.
0                  Beginning in 1937, many intellectuals, passing themselves
0
              off as refugees, migrated to the United States.     Most came 'from

0             central and southern Europe.     They were generally Marxist-

              Socialist oriented and were fleeing their homeland because of the
0             looming Nazi-Fascist terror.     They entered many universities as

              professors and lecturers and our government in a favored status,
0             as P-12s, without security clearances.     They spawned much of our


0       Li   '..liberal media and future government employees.   I pulled many of

             'their files and they advanced by recommending each other.       Their

              thinking favoring communism continued to influence not only World
0             War II but Korea and Vietnam.
0
                   In later years I met Winston Churchill and shook hands with


0               him.     He had come to Rome to review the plans for the invasion of

                southern France.

0

        I received a call from the Commanding General, and he
                                                                        C)
instructed me to report to a suite on the top floor of the              C)
Excelsior Hotel.     I was to take my weapon and report to a General   • 0
                                                                        C)
Browning.    He. said, "So Charley Dasher sent his Chief of Security
                                                                         0
 and Intelligence."    When I leave, I will lock you in, give me         0
 your left wrist."    I extended it, and he snapped a handcuff and       C)

chain from which hung a dispatch briefcase.    "I'll unlock you
                                                                         C)
tomorrow."    I stayed up all night guarding the plans for the

invasion of Southern France.     In the morning, a group of officers     0
                                                                         0
and civilians came in.    General Browning took the briefcase.     I
                                                                             0
. was introduced to one of the civilians.   A stocky man, smoking a
                                                                             0
cigar      he was Winston Churchill.                                         C)

                 Just after the surrender of the German Armies, the United

           States Government began to deport Mafia or "Cosa Nostra"

           characters from the United States to Italy.
0                                                        General Pasquale the

           Chief of Italian Intelligence, once told me, "Corso, don't we
0          have enough of these types in Italy, and you ship us more."

           Jokingly, I answered, "Well, they are returning to the land of

0          their roots, you know often we are very benevolent."    This was

           always good for a refill of good Italian wine.   We placed them on

           a watch list.
0
                 One of these types, made himself a United States Army

    L.     Colonel, and went to the huge sprawling building of the Allied
0          Control Commission.   He found a suite of empty offices and set

           himself up in business.   He hired a secretary and other   '

0   1_
           civilians, hung up a shingle and painted an official looking sign

           and his name on the glass door panel.   He began to sell United

          States surplus military material. I received a series of
0
0         complaints whereby he had collected millions of lire in advances,

           but no deliveries were forthcoming.   I moved in before he moved
    .r1
          out.    His explapation was, "They were greedy bloodsuckers, and
0
0         what did I have to lose."    Another, familiar with the military.
0         'syttem, wrote himself military orders as a Captain.    He was

          having a merry time traveling from base to base on military

          travel orders, he was collecting pay and allowances and even
0         travel pay. )1e had PX ration cards and was making a mint on the

          black market.    I caught him in the nick of time, he had promoted
1 49                                                                             "jC
        himself to Major and was planning to write himself orders

        transferring back to the States.     He had $25,000.00 on him and

        had defrauded the United States Army of a similar amount.

               We used to laugh and felt that /such ingenuity had to be

        admired.

               Shortly afterwards and following a successful operation      WY
        C.I.C. Captain's invited me to a dinner at one of' Rome's most

        picturesque restaurants.    Perched on a hill overlooking Rome, I

        arrived with Franco, my driver, bodyguard and Customs Lieutenant.

        When I arrived the C.I.C. officers stood up.     On occasions like

        this I often wondered, strange things happen in the military,

        among those standing were two FBI agents, a New York Detective, a

        Boston Detective and a Chicago Police Lieutenant.     These were'all

        professionals, and highly trained.    I was the least professional,

        but the highest military rank and so I was their operational

        commander.    A mutual respect had grown between us, because not

        only shared the good times; but also shared in the dangers of our

        job.    I was proud that they accepted me as their leader.

               When I sat down, eight men at the next table arose in unison

        and departed leaving unfinished meals.    Dominick Zullo, one of my

        best agents, the New York Chief of Detectives spoke up and said,

        "Colonel did you know who was in that group?     When he saw you, he

        recognized you and left and his cohorts followed."     I said, "No;

       'Dominick, the one with glasses looked vaguely familiar.
        .

               Zullo said, "That was Lucky Luciano."   I came back, "Well,

        he must know about us, so Dominick since, you have been

        acquainted in the past, contact him and give him this message, I

        have no truck with him.    lie can remain in Rome, but under no
     11   conditions will he conduct any operation in my area.     Make it

          short and sweet, he will understand."
     11        I never heard, or saw hide or hair of him again.

                                  CLARK GABLE

                                      AND

                                HUMPHREY BOGART


            In early 1943, I left for England.    Our flight left Andrews

    Air Force Base and that afternoon we landed at Dover Air Force

    Base in Maine.    That night after dinner we went to the officer's

    club.    It was a hugh hanger type building.     Hundreds of Air Force
A   Officers were there and the drinking and singing was heavy and

    loud.     An announcement came over the loudspeaker that Captain

    Clark Gable, was buying the drinks for the house for the rest of

    the night.    A loud cheer went up.     Later in the evening, he

    stopped at our table and he was a most delightful individual.              He

    shook hands with us and wished us all good luck.       The men in his

    squadron swore by him and said that he was a real man and a nice

    fellow.    They thought the world of him and so did we, a real down

    to earth officer.    Possibly many were influenced by the drinks,

    but I had the impression that he was a most sincere and friendly

    individual.

         Although we were deeply involved in military matters the

    highlights of this short narrative is my meeting with Clark

    Gable, and Humphrey Bogart.    Even as hardened individuals as we

    were becoming, we recognized the two as down to earth, likable

    individuals with which we could relate in. this stage of a war

    time setting.    We were in the scenario with them.

         After an eventful tour in England I left with my

    interrogation team on a C-54 headed for fifth army headquarters.
                                                                                    •
                                                                       I   .
                                                                                                       t el

         We arrived deep in Africa at Marrakech, took a truck over the

         Atlas Mountains, to Algiers, then to Mostaganem and Army

         Headquarters. We began preparing for the invasion on Italy.

         After the landing at Salerno we held and moved north, took

         Naples, moved north to Caserta and set up in the former

         headquarters of the Italien Air Force Academy.    Fifth army

         headquarters was a relatively comfortable place after sleeping on

         the ground and in tents for months. I had a room on the fifth

        floor.     A friend of mine Captain Pat Romano had just left the

       . forty-fifth division for assignment at headquarters and I asked

         to share my room.    Which he did along with the fleas he brought

         with him from sleeping on the ground.

kht             While staying at fifth army headquarters, at the Air Academy

 ti     Building, I met Humphrey Bogart. It wasn't a planned meeting, but
S14.     very informal and in a way embarrassing.    The first time I saw
1 I
        him he was sitting on his rear knocked down by a swinging door.

        Captain Romano and I, had been together before, in the States and

        Italy.    He was a sound sleeper and I had trouble getting him up

        in the morning.    The doors in the Officer's mess closed at 7:30

       .sharp and unless you entered before you went without breakfast.

        This particular morning we were close to the deadline.     From the

        fifth floor there was a winding spiral stairs that led to the

        rear doors of the mess hall.     We only had a few minutes and were

       'flying down the stairs: . Pat- was over 6' and went well over 200

       : lbs.   He waS leading.   He hit the swinging door at full speed and

        we heard a thud!.    I hit the other door.   We saw.a man sitting on

        the floor. and realized we had knocked him down.    We quickly went

        oVerHand helped him to his feet and apologized.     I saw a smiling
                     •

                                                                    •


                                                                                                 ...
                                                                                        •
                                                                                    •
                                                                                •           ..
face and recognized the voice aa he said, "I should have learned

long ago not to get in front of a swinging door in a bar or mess

hall."   I thought, "The screen's toughest man apologizing to two

sleepy, Junior army officers, I said, "I am sorry Mr. Bogart, we

overslept and were hurrying without thinking."   "Your not hurt?"

He answered, "A little dirt in the rear never hurt anyone."       Then

we all three began to laugh and I said, "Mr Bogart, will you let

us buy you a drink tonight at the bar?"   His answer was, "I

accept your invitation a little alcohol will ease any bruises."

I said, Yes, Sir, we will go to the standing bar."   He laughed

and we shook hands.

     We made breakfast thanks to Humphrey Bogart, because

everything even serving stopped until we got him on his feet,

                             HATA HARI?


       There is one observation I wish to make.   The deadly game I
played was nothing more than an extension of the deadly combat

war game that was being played by the allied armies on the battle

field.    Except for collaborators of which I arrested over 200,

like Axis Sally there were no women involved on either side.        In
espionage and sabotage there was no such thing as a Matn Hari.

We used no female agents and neither did the Germans.

       At one, point I was sitting at dinner with the Chief of

Italian Intelligence, General De Pasquale, in a joking manner I

said, "General, I thought I we's a friend of yours."    He said.,

"You are, have we done something wrong?"     I answered, "Yes." "In

the movies and story books, an intelligence service, targets

their counterpart, and sends a sultry, beautiful spy to seduce

him.    What is the matter with me?   Send one to ensnare me and if

she is pretty enough, I will talk my head off."     He began to

laugh and said, "We will see what can be done."

       I doubt if we will be called male chauvinist, because we

excluded women into his dirty game of kill or be killed.     I don't

think that even women's lib will complain because we left them

out of this rotten game we called intelligence and security and

counter espionage and sabotage.

       One night while I was at dinner with Karl Haas, my counter

part in the German Army.   I asked him, "Karl, why didn't you use

any female .agents against us?   I destroyed your male agents, why

didn't you try a female, perhaps you would have caught us off
                                                                          CD
     guard?"   He laughed and said, "What a waste of time and effort it   C)
     would have been to train women to seduce men like you in Italy.      CD
     A country that is overflowing with beautiful women. How could we     CD
                                                                          C)
     compete against such a bounty."
                                                                          CD
          We did use some of the administrative personnel that worked     C)
     at our C.I.C. headquarters.   For example, when an agent was         C)
                                                                          C)
     suppose to deliver crystals to the   two agents transmitting
                                                                          0
     messages from Rome to the Germans.   The rondevous was in the        C)
     lovers lane area of Villa Borghese, the hugh public park in the      C)
                                                                          C)
     center of Rome.   However, since we did not have enough girls, we
                                                                          C)
     had to dreds about a dozen male agents in woman's clothes.           C)
          It was a successful operation and we captured the German        C)

     agents.   I won't mention the sly and lewd remarks made by the
                                                                          CD

     agents.   I even made observations that dome of them made the most

     ugly looking females I had ever seen.                                0

                                                     TEMPTATIONS -hiOntrieBELDS


                                                             An intelligence operator in a foreign country during a war

                                                     can become a pinnacle of power.    Control of investigations,

                                                     authority to arrest and release, the exercise of military

                                                     authority, and control of indigenous security forces can become

                                                     an awesome power centered in one individual.    This power brings

                                                     with it the seeking of favors by those caught in the security

                                                     net, which can be easily accepted or rejected.     Early in my tour

                                                     in Rome I had to make the decision on which path to follow.     The

                                                     beginning was the attempted bribes with gold coins to release

                                                     prisoners from my security prison.

                                                       .   Perhaps most fortunate, the protection of the lives of the

                                              !i I   Americans in the area and security of our installation became

                                                     almost an obsession bordering on religious fervor with me.      As I

                                                     look back, my power and authority stemmed mainly from this one

                                                     fact.    I was constantly under attack from the communists,

                                                     British, Socialists, many Italians,and even high American

                                                     officials whose skirts were not always clean.    I was difficult to

                                                     get at, and was always ready to shove fabricated information down
                                              '71    the throats of the originators.    I could give many examples, but

                                                     I want to cover one of the most lucrative temptations ever

                                                     offered to me.

                                                           During the first two years I had a most interesting and

                                                     intelligent Italian working for me.    Sometime in 1946, he called

                                                     and asked me to come down to his place of business.    I went, and

                                                     it was a very elaborate antique shop.    He greeted me cordially,
                                                                       -

and we went back into the back room where he showed me a painting

of Christ on the cross done by Titian.      Next, two Mongolian
                                                                           C)
ponies about two feet high and three feet long.    Lastly, was the         C)
real treasure -- a small bronze statue covered with patina, by             C)
Cellini.
                                                                           C)
     Then he began, "Senor Corso, if there is one man I trust in

this world, it is you.   I will pack the Mongolian ponies, which

date back to Genghis Khan; they will be suspended in the box.      I
                                                                           0
will pack the Titian and Cellini statues., I will make bills of
                                                                           C)
sale for you.   Then in ten or fifteen years you will surface them         0
and offer them for sale.    We will be partners in this venture."          C)
    It was a kind of a shock to me and I said, "Aldo, the
                                                                           C)
Cellini is a priceless national treasure.    Can you imagine the           C)
furor it would cause when it would be surfaced in the United               0
States?"   He said, "Yes, I know, but you can handle it."
                                                                           C)
                                                                           C)
    "Aldo, at the moment I have to say no, but I thank' you for

your faith in me."

    He answered, "I was afraid you would say that, but think
                                                                           C)
                                                                           C)
about it; my offer stands    indefinitely, for you only."
                                                                           C)

         My future was enhanced by an operation which I recommended'

     but was never ordered to be carried out.   At a staff meeting in       "39


      which the Theater Commander was present, he asked me, "Corso, how

TI    is the Communist Party being financed?"

           I answered, "By the Soviet Union."

           He came back with the question, "Can you prove what you just

      said?"

           "Yes, Sir."

           He then said, "If you can prove it, why have you never

      submitted the evidence?"

           T replied, "For the reason, Sir, that I need an order from
      higher headquarters before I can furnish the last piece of

      indisputable evidence."

           "What order do you need?"

           I continued, "My black market informants tell me the exact

      day that Russian gold will hit the market, even to the point of

      which plane it will arrive on.     I know the Soviet Embassy cars

      that go to pick up the couriers.    There is a curve on the road

      the cars have to take.    This curve is along side of a cliff so at

      that point the cars will slow up.   The cars will be waylaid;

      then, with the occupants in them, will be pushed over the cliff.

      The carrying case containing the gold bullion will be delivered

      to me, and I will bring it you unopened."

           He shot back, "But we don't do such things."

           I answered back, "But they do, Sir, and it is the only way."

           He ended the conversation by saying, "I will let you know."

           A week later, General Dasher called me and said, "Phil, here

      is a letter and a certificate for you, which I am most pleased to

      give you."   In it was the First Army Commemoration Medal given in

      Italy and personally signed by General Lee, the Theater
i
t


i]
      Commander.   It was accompanied by a letter which said:

          "In the future, there will be ekams to qualify reserve

      officers for the Regular Army.   I an personally sending a letter

      to Washington recommending you for appointment to the Regular

      Army."

          General Dasher shook hands with me and said, "My endorsement

      goes with the letter."

     interesting and icing reaching effects.

                                 FAREWELL TO ROME AND ITALY
                                                      geM A IA/ s Ft, 1 7'A   y
                             7116 "ogvii_ /1A111c6

                Because of my school days when Latin was ay favorite
0          subject, I love to say that I found the city of the Caesars,
0
0          Eternal Rome, in shambles; and in restoring law and order, it

           started back to its former glory.              I often ask myself, did I

           really do this or would it have come back without me?                     So I will

           take some credit for maybe accelerating its comeback.                     Really,

           all I did was to keep events on the right track, allowing no
0   -{ I   interference.    But I do now know that many Italians appreciated

           what I did.
0   t771
0   i41
                After my last meeting with C.I.G's Angleton, I left Rome for
0          good and returned to Livorno.             The afternoon before my departure,

           my phone rang; and the desk attendant said there were two

0          gentlemen to see me, Senor Vigorelli, my partisan friend, who had

           worked with me along with his men, was in the lobby.                     I went down
0
           immediately.    He embraced me and introduced me to his friend,

           whom I had never met, one of the top industrialists and
0          wealthiest men in. Italy. (He said he was obligated to me for

           curbing the power of the CGIL, the communist Labor Union.)

0               I asked Vigorelli, "How did you find me?"                     His answer was,

           'We always know where our friends are."
0               They came to invite me; on my last night in Italy, to a
0
0          dinner at the home of the Admiral in command of the Naval
0          Academy; just outside of Livorno.


0
                                                                                 .15-
                                                                                        0

                                                                                        0
           That evening an official car called for me and my wife.          We
                                                             •                          0
      arrived, and an honor guard of midshipmen greeted us. There

      were about twenty guests.
                                                                                        0
                                    The Admiral and his wife were most

      gracious.    At the dinner, after the usual toasts and plaudits,

      asked Vigorelli, "Are they really talking about me?"      He answered             0
      seriously, "Yes."
                                                                                        0
           The Admiral made the final toast and thanked me for what I
                                                                                        0
      had done for his country and that I had to return some day.

      Finally, I was called upon to speak.     It was fortunate that after
                                                                                        0
      four years, I had learned to converse well in Italian.     I arose

      and signaled to one of the attendants to bring me the package I

      had brought.    I began:
                                                                                        0
           "My dear friends:     I leave with only the regrets that I may

      never see you again.    I especially want to single out Senor                         0
ri!   Vigorelli.     We went through many trying times, and I know how                      0
      many times he looked out for me.     And his presence at my side
                                                                                            0
      struck fear into the hearts of my enemies and those of Italy.         I

      am going home; my war is over, and we defeated our enemy and                          0
      dispersed Italy's enemies. .But, your war is not over.      You have

      a dangerous enemy lurking over your border., and many are in your

      midst."   (I picked up the package and continued.)    "I have       here

      my personal combat gear with which I fought our enemies.        I

      entrust this weapon, with which I defended my Country, to my

      Olose and loyal friend, Vigorelli."    I turned to him, "It is

      yours to use in defense of your Country.     I will always have a

      warm feeling in my heart knowing I.entrusted it to you as a

      symbol of our friendship."

           I handed him the package.     He opened it.   In it was my


                                                                                            'C
                                               ql


000000000000000000000000 0000000000000000000
                                                     combat webbed belt, hanging on it were my ammo clips, f        t aid


                                               fl    kit, canteen, Marine Stilleto, and holster containing my .45

                                                     caliber pistol.   He stared nt it; and this tough partisan

                                               111   fighter, overcome- with emotion began to cry.    He composed
                                               a
                                               lc    himself, came over and embraced me tightly, then said, "I'll

                                                     never disgrace this gift.    It will be my most precious possession

                                                     and forever remain in my family."

                                                          The other guests applauded, and each in turn embraced me and

                                                     kissed my wife's hand.   As we were leaving, it took me hours to

                                                     explain to my wife, who did not understand Italian, what had

                                                     transpired.

                                                          And so I departed Italy, leaving behind many friends and

                                                     taking with me fond memories.

                                                          Senor Vigorelli, was a fitting heir for my combat gun and

                                                     knife.   The man I gave my .45 pistol and the Stilleto was the

                                                     most feared Italian partisan of the war.     The Germans were never

                                                     able to capture him and he created havoc behind their lines.           He

                                                     also hated the communist and treated them as enemies.     He showed

                                                     up in my office one day and said, "I want to shake the hand of

                                                     the American officer who the    say has the eyes of the devil and

                                                     my enemies fear more than they do me."     He continued "How can we

                                                     help?"   I said Senor, "I won't ask you to work for me, but to

                                                     join me and work with me."   He responded, "That is why we are

                                                     here."   I gave him Mussolini's armored sedan, G-2 passes and

                                                     letters addressed to all American units.    Even General Dasher

                                                     heard from military units, of as they said, "Cutthroats" with

                                                     Rome area military passes.   I laughed and so did the General.

                                                     "When I said, "That's correct Sir, they are MY "Cutthroats."           I
                                                                                           •   Ai, -


     later found out that Vigorelli sent word to the communists,

     "Touch my friend Corso and it will be worse than the Ardentine
     cave massacre for you communists!"

7)                                             AND
7)                                     ITALIAN AUXILIARIES
7)
7)
                  During the discussion of my accomplishments in Rome, I would

             be remiss if I did, not give the proper credit and recognition to

             my C.I.C. agents.     They were highly. - trained professionals and
CD
CD           were a match for their counterparts anywhere in the World.       These
CD           men were not the Marxist -trained ideologist of OSS and CIG, but
CD           Americans proudly wearing the uniform of the United States Army.
CD
CD                At their farewell party they gave me, I announced that

CD           General Charles Dasher had endorsed my recommendation, in each
CD     TI    case, that they be promoted ahead of their contemporaries.       In
CD
CD
CD
       1,1   addition, a short profile and list of accomplishments had been

             forwarded to the Pentagon, along with each endorsement.
 CD               Then came the difficult part of saying goodbye to the loyal
 CD
             Italians assigned to my staff.     They were at my farewell party.
 CD
 CD          First lined up were the 12 Custom guards.       I announced that, "You
 CD          will all return to your Service, and each will be promoted one
 CD          grade or rank.   A grateful United States Army has given each of
 CD
 CD          you the highest decoration it could award to foreigners, the
 CD          Medal of Freedom.     As for me, my admiration and thoughts will
 CD          always be with you.    Then I started down the' line.   Each snapped
 CD
 CD          at attention as I stood before him.     I addressed each one. "When
 CD          I took this, job, never did I dream that such men as you would
 CD          stand steadfast with me."
  CD
  CD
  CD
  CD
  CD
  CD
      Angleo       -   The will of the wisp. You could not be
                       found, but you were always there. I
                       always saw you hovering to protect my
                       back.

      Giovanni     -   The only man that while on surveillance
                       sold cigarettes and never made any
                       money. Goodbye, my friend.
      Pasquale     -   The brave sergeant who always was in the
                       front when there was danger. When we
                       arrested the saboteurs, you made an ugly
                       woman in female clothes; but I admire
                       you.

      Sebastiano       The entrepreneur who sold watermelon
                       slices and chestnuts while on guard
                       watching our enemies.

      Giuseppe         You could be a cat burglar. You always
                       surprised our enemies from on top of a
,t+
                       building or above them.

      Antonio      -   The World's best driver.   No automobile
6-                     could escape you.

      Paolo        -   In Rome's crowded street, no person
                       could lose you. You are the best.

      Vincenzo     -   My expert marksman; you never missed

      Luigi        -   The only man that could teach me how to
                       use a knife. My private messenger.

      Aldo             You could blow a door or hole in a wall
                       better than any explosive expert. You
                       are the best.

      Lt. Franco       And finally, my brave and valiant
                       Franco, I entrusted my family's safety
                       to you. How many long hours you watched
                       alertly over me at night or early
                       mornings. Above all, how many times you
                       stepped in front of me to take any
                       bullet intended for me. This I will
                       never forget. I embraced him and said,
                       'You have been promoted. I will be the
                       first to address you as Captain.'

      Visconti     -   My carabiniere strong man who kept order
                       in my prison. I said, 'When you were at
                       my side, I felt that Hercules walked
                       with me.' He was 6' 7" and had to stoop
                       over to embrace me.
